    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"pdf_page\": 31,\n\
      \  \"review_status\": \"pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"178\\n       BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157\
      \       Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &\\n                                                         \
      \                     Technical Specifications\\n\\n\\n              b)   Cantilevers – A  cantilever  type\
      \  of  construction depends on  the  balancing\\n            superimposed structure for its stability. Canopies,\
      \ cornices, staircases, balconies should\\n           be demolished or supported before the balancing load\
      \ is removed.\\n               c)  Heavy floor beams – Heavy bulks of timber should be supported before cutting\
      \ at\\n             the extremities and should then be lowered to a safe working place.\\n\\n            \
      \ 3.12  Removal of materials\\n\\n            3.12.1  General.     Removal of dismantled materials should\
      \ be done carefully; they\\n         may be thrown / lowered to the ground. The materials shall preferably\
      \ be dumped inside\\n            the building. Normally such materials shall be lowered to the ground or to\
      \ the top of the\\n            sidewalk shed where provided by means of ropes or suitable tackles.\\n    \
      \        3.12.2  Through chutes\\n\\n               a.   Wooden or metal shall be provided for removal of\
      \ materials.  The chutes shall\\n              preferably be provided at the center of the building for efficient\
      \ disposal of debris.\\n\\n                   b.  Chutes if provided at an angle of more than 45 degree from\
      \ the horizontal shall\\n                 be entirely enclosed on all sides, except for opening at or about\
      \ the floor level for\\n                     receiving materials.\\n                    c.  Opening for chutes\
      \ shall not exceed 1.20 m in height measured along the wall of\\n                    the chute and in all\
      \ storeys below the top floor such opening shall be kept closed\\n                when not in use.\\n    \
      \               d. To prevent the descending material attaining a dangerous speed, the chute shall\\n    \
      \                not extend in an unbroken line for more than two storeys. A gate or step shall be\\n    \
      \                provided with suitable means of closing at the bottom of each chute to stop the\\n      \
      \               flow of materials.\\n                   e.  Chutes at an angle less than 45 degree to the\
      \ horizontal may be left open on the\\n                   upper side provided that at the point where such\
      \ chute discharges into the chute\\n                    steeper than 45 degree to the horizontal, the top\
      \ of the steeper chute shall be\\n                  boarded over to prevent the escape of materials.\\n  \
      \                          f.  Any opening into which workmen dump debris at the top of the chute shall be\\\
      n                  guarded by a substantial guard rail extending at least 1 m above the level of the\\n  \
      \                      floor or other surface on which men stand to dump the materials into the chute.\\n\
      \                   g. A toe board or bumper not less than 50 mm thick and 150 mm high shall be\\n       \
      \             provided at each chute opening, if the required material is dumped from the wheel\\n       \
      \             barrows. Any space between the chute and the edge of the opening in the floor\\n           \
      \         through which it passes shall be solidly planked over.\\n\\n             3.12.3 Through openings\\\
      n\\n                  a)  Debris may also be dropped through holes in the floor without the use of chutes.\\\
      n                       In such a case the total area of the hole cut in the intermediate floor, one which\\\
      n                         lies between floor that is being demolished and the storage floor shall not exceed\\\
      n                 25 per cent of such floor area.  It shall be ensured that the storage floor is of\\n   \
      \               adequate strength to withstand the impact of the falling material.\\n                  b)\
      \ Openings in all floors below the floor from which materials are being removed,\\n                      \
      \ shall be protected by standard railings and toe boards (see IS 4912: 1978) or\\n                     preferably\
      \ planked over if the holes are not being used for dumping materials.\\n                   c)  All intermediate\
      \ floor openings for passage of materials shall be completely closed\\n                     with barricades\
      \ or guard rails not less than 1 m high and at a distance of not less\\n                   than 1 m from the\
      \ edge of the general opening. No barricades or guard rails shall\\n                 be removed until the\
      \ storey immediately above has been demolished down to the\\n                        floor line and all debris\
      \ cleared from the floor.\\n\\n\\n\\n\\n       Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                 September\
      \ 2026           Section Page 28 of 37\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.99940490722656,\n\

      : 0,\n      \"text\": \" BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157              Employer Requirement,\
      \ Scope of Work &  \\n \\n \\n Technical Specifications \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.99996948242188,\n\
      \        787.1278076171875,\n        538.54248046875,\n        795.56982421875\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 1,\n      \"text\": \"Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd. \\n \\nSeptember 2026                    Section\

      : \" \\n \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        72.00048828125,\n        74.16320037841797,\n\

      : \"b) \\nCantilevers – A cantilever type of construction depends on the balancing \\nsuperimposed structure\
      \ for its stability.  Canopies, cornices, staircases, balconies should \\nbe demolished or supported before\
      \ the balancing load is removed. \\nc) \\nHeavy floor beams – Heavy bulks of timber should be supported before\
      \ cutting at \\nthe extremities and should then be lowered to a safe working place. \\n \\n\"\n    },\n  \
      \  {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        102.00042724609375,\n        149.883056640625,\n        260.8294982910156,\n\
      \        172.9585418701172\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 4,\n      \"text\": \"3.12 \\nRemoval of materials\
      \  \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        72.00038146972656,\n        175.2030029296875,\n \

      : \"3.12.1 General. \\nRemoval of dismantled materials should be done carefully; they \\nmay be thrown / lowered\
      \ to the ground. The materials shall preferably be dumped inside \\nthe building. Normally such materials\

      \ suitable tackles.  \\n3.12.2 Through chutes  \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        108.00032043457031,\n\

      : 6,\n      \"text\": \"a. \\nWooden or metal shall be provided for removal of materials.  The chutes shall\
      \ \\npreferably be provided at the center of the building for efficient disposal of debris. \\n\"\n    },\n\
      \    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        126.00052642822266,\n        276.60284423828125,\n        539.2274780273438,\n\
      \        299.5583190917969\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 7,\n      \"text\": \" \\nb. Chutes if provided\
      \ at an angle of more than 45 degree from the horizontal shall \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n \
      \       125.99970245361328,\n        301.80279541015625,\n        539.2273559570312,\n        337.4782409667969\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 8,\n      \"text\": \"be entirely enclosed on all sides, except for opening\
      \ at or about the floor level for \\nreceiving materials. \\nc. Opening for chutes shall not exceed 1.20 m\
      \ in height measured along the wall of \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        125.9999008178711,\n\
      \        339.8427429199219,\n        539.2274780273438,\n        375.3982238769531\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 9,\n      \"text\": \"the chute and in all storeys below the top floor such opening shall be kept closed\
      \ \\nwhen not in use. \\nd. To prevent the descending material attaining a dangerous speed, the chute shall\
      \ \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        126.00016021728516,\n        377.7627258300781,\n     \
      \   539.2273559570312,\n        426.0382385253906\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 10,\n      \"text\":\
      \ \"not extend in an unbroken line for more than two storeys. A gate or step shall be \\nprovided with suitable\
      \ means of closing at the bottom of each chute to stop the \\nflow of materials. \\ne. Chutes at an angle\
      \ less than 45 degree to the horizontal may be left open on the \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n\
      \        125.9993667602539,\n        428.2827453613281,\n        539.22705078125,\n        476.67822265625\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 11,\n      \"text\": \"upper side provided that at the point where such\
      \ chute discharges into the chute \\nsteeper than 45 degree to the horizontal, the top of the steeper chute\
      \ shall be \\nboarded over to prevent the escape of materials. \\nf. Any opening into which workmen dump debris\
      \ at the top of the chute shall be \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        125.99942779541016,\n\
      \        478.9227294921875,\n        539.2266235351562,\n        514.5982666015625\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 12,\n      \"text\": \"guarded by a substantial guard rail extending at least 1 m above the level of the\
      \ \\nfloor or other surface on which men stand to dump the materials into the chute. \\ng. A toe board or\
      \ bumper not less than 50 mm thick and 150 mm high shall be \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n    \
      \    71.99874877929688,\n        516.8427124023438,\n        539.2257080078125,\n        564.9982299804688\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 13,\n      \"text\": \"provided at each chute opening, if the required\
      \ material is dumped from the wheel \\nbarrows. Any space between the chute and the edge of the opening in\
      \ the floor \\nthrough which it passes shall be solidly planked over. \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\"\
      : [\n        71.99874877929688,\n        567.3627319335938,\n        246.6644287109375,\n        590.3182373046875\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 14,\n      \"text\": \"3.12.3 Through openings  \\n \\n\"\n    },\n   \
      \ {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        125.99871063232422,\n        592.802734375,\n        539.2263793945312,\n\
      \        603.1582641601562\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 15,\n      \"text\": \"a) Debris may also be\
      \ dropped through holes in the floor without the use of chutes. \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n\
      \        125.9987564086914,\n        605.4027099609375,\n        539.2265014648438,\n        666.3982543945312\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 16,\n      \"text\": \"In such a case the total area of the hole cut in\
      \ the intermediate floor, one which \\nlies between floor that is being demolished and the storage floor shall\
      \ not exceed \\n25 per cent of such floor area. It shall be ensured that the storage floor is of \\nadequate\
      \ strength to withstand the impact of the falling material. \\nb) Openings in all floors below the floor from\
      \ which materials are being removed, \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        125.99976348876953,\n\
      \        668.6427001953125,\n        539.2266845703125,\n        704.3182373046875\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 17,\n      \"text\": \"shall be protected by standard railings and toe boards (see IS 4912: 1978) or \\\
      npreferably planked over if the holes are not being used for dumping materials. \\nc) All intermediate floor\
      \ openings for passage of materials shall be completely closed \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n \
      \       143.99996948242188,\n        706.5626831054688,\n        539.2274169921875,\n        754.9581909179688\n\

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"pdf_page\": 32,\n\
      \  \"review_status\": \"pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"179\\n BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157\
      \       Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &\\n                                                         \
      \              Technical Specifications\\n\\n\\n           d) When cutting a hole in an intermediate floor,\
      \ between the storage floor and the\\n                 floor which is being demolished, makes the intermediate\
      \ floor or any portion of it\\n              unsafe, then such intermediate floor shall be properly shored.\
      \  It shall also be\\n             ensured that the supporting walls are not kept without adequate lateral\
      \ restraints.\\n\\n       3.13  References\\n\\n       Other Indian Standards on the subject of safety of\
      \ workers are as follows:\\n\\n                 IS No.                                        Title\\n   \
      \     3696 (Part 1): 1987      Safety code of scaffolds and ladders Part 1 Scaffolds\\n\\n        4014 (Part\
      \ 2): 1967     Code of practice for steel tubular scaffolding ; Part 2 Safety\\n                         \
      \          regulations for scaffolding\\n\\n         3764: 1992           Code of safety for excavation work\
      \ (first revision)\\n\\n         7969: 1975             Safety code for handling and storage of building materials.\\\
      n\\n         13415: 1992          Code  of  safety  for  protective  barriers  in and around\\n          \
      \                           buildings.\\n\\n        13416 (Part-1): 1992    Recommendations   for  preventive\
      \  measures  against\\n                               hazards at workplaces; Part 1 Falling material hazards\\\
      n                                   prevention.\\n\\n        13416 (Part 2): 1982   Recommendations   for\
      \  preventive  measures  against\\n                               hazards at work places; Part 2 Fall prevention.\\\
      n\\n         13430: 1992          Code of practice for safety during additional construction\\n          \
      \                  and alteration to existing buildings.\\n\\n\\n\\n\\n\\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation\
      \ Ltd.                 September 2026           Section Page 29 of 37\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n  \
      \    \"bbox\": [\n        71.99940490722656,\n        37.728336334228516,\n        526.5408935546875,\n  \
      \      56.49034118652344\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 0,\n      \"text\": \" BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157\
      \              Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &  \\n \\n \\n Technical Specifications \\n\"\n    },\n\
      \    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.99996948242188,\n        787.1278076171875,\n        538.54248046875,\n\
      \        795.56982421875\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 1,\n      \"text\": \"Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation\
      \ Ltd. \\n \\nSeptember 2026                    Section Page 29 of 37 \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\"\
      : [\n        71.99996948242188,\n        798.4948120117188,\n        538.4083251953125,\n        826.9197998046875\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 2,\n      \"text\": \" \\n \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n\
      \        125.99994659423828,\n        74.16320037841797,\n        539.2267456054688,\n        84.51871490478516\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 3,\n      \"text\": \"d) When cutting a hole in an intermediate floor,\
      \ between the storage floor and the \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.99932861328125,\n\
      \        86.76317596435547,\n        542.2265014648438,\n        135.1586151123047\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 4,\n      \"text\": \"floor which is being demolished, makes the intermediate floor or any portion of it\
      \ \\nunsafe, then such intermediate floor shall be properly shored. It shall also be \\nensured that the supporting\
      \ walls are not kept without adequate lateral restraints.  \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n \
      \       71.99932861328125,\n        137.2830810546875,\n        469.50628662109375,\n        185.67857360839844\n\

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"pdf_page\": 30,\n\
      \  \"review_status\": \"pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"177\\n BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157\
      \       Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &\\n                                                         \
      \              Technical Specifications\\n\\n\\n\\n           1) Beams – For beams supporting rope should\
      \ be attached to the beam. Then the\\n              concrete should be removed from both ends by pneumatic\
      \ drill or cutting tools\\n            and the reinforcement exposed. The reinforcement should then be cut\
      \ in which a\\n           way as to allow the beam to be lowered under control to the floor.\\n          \
      \ 2) Columns – For columns reinforcement should be exposed at the base after\\n               restraining\
      \ wire guy ropes have been placed around the member at the top. The\\n              reinforcement should then\
      \ be cut in such a way as to allow the column be pulled\\n           down to the floor under control.\\n \
      \          3) Walls – Reinforced concrete walls should be cut into strips and demolished as for\\n       \
      \      columns.\\n           4) Suspended floors and roofs – where ribbed construction is used, the principle\\\
      n                of design and method of construction should be ascertained before demolition.\\n        \
      \    Care should be taken not to cut the ribs inadvertently.\\n\\n       3.11.4 Pre-cast reinforced concrete\\\
      n\\n       a) Pre-cast reinforced concrete units in a structure are normally held in position by the\\n  \
      \         strength of the joints in-situ or on supporting walls, etc. As such before starting on\\n      \
      \     demolition the joint structures or the supporting mechanisms shall be studied and\\n          understood.\\\
      n       b) In devising the following demolition sequences, due precaution shall be taken to avoid\\n     \
      \      toppling over of the prefabricated units or any other part of the structure and wherever\\n       \
      \   necessary temporary supports shall be provided.\\n\\n       3.11.5 Pre-stressed concrete.     Before \
      \ commencing  of  the  demolition  work\\n        involving such structures advice of an expert engineer should\
      \ be obtained.\\n\\n       3.11.6 Steel\\n\\n       a)    No beams shall be cut until precautions have been\
      \ taking to prevent  it from\\n       swinging freely and possibly striking any worker or equipment or any\
      \ part of the structure\\n       being demolished.\\n       b)      All structural steel members shall be\
      \ lowered from the building and shall not be\\n       allowed to drop.\\n        c)    Tag lines shall be\
      \ used on  all materials being lowered or hoisted up and a\\n       standard signal system shall be used and\
      \ workmen instructed on the signals. No person\\n        shall be permitted to ride the load line.\\n    \
      \   d)    When a derrick or hoisting equipment is used care shall be taken to see that the\\n        floor\
      \ on which it is supported shall be strong enough for the loading.  If necessary heavy\\n       planking shall\
      \ be used to distribute the load to floor beams and girders. Overloading /\\n       overturning of the equipment\
      \ shall be avoided.\\n\\n       3.11.7 Other elements\\n\\n       a)     Roof trusses – Roof trusses shall\
      \ be removed to wall plate level by hand methods.\\n        Sufficient purlins and bracing should be retained\
      \ to ensure stability of the remaining roof\\n       trusses while each individual truss is removed. Temporary\
      \ bracing should be added,\\n      where necessary, to minimize instability. The end frame opposite to the\
      \ end where\\n       dismantling is commenced, or a convenient intermediate frame should be independently\\\
      n      and securely guyed in both directions before work starts. On no account should the\\n      bottom tie\
      \ of a truss be cut until the principal rafters are prevented from making outward\\n      movement.\\n\\n\\\
      n\\n\\n\\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                 September 2026           Section Page 27 of\
      \ 37\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.99940490722656,\n        37.728336334228516,\n\
      \        526.5408935546875,\n        56.49034118652344\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 0,\n      \"text\"\
      : \" BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157              Employer Requirement, Scope of Work & \
      \ \\n \\n \\n Technical Specifications \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.99996948242188,\n\
      \        787.1278076171875,\n        538.54248046875,\n        795.56982421875\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\

      : 9,\n      \"text\": \"3.11.5 Pre-stressed concrete. \\nBefore commencing of the demolition work \\ninvolving\
      \ such structures advice of an expert engineer should be obtained. \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\"\

      bbox\": [\n        72.0,\n        453.60272216796875,\n        539.2273559570312,\n        615.7582397460938\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 11,\n      \"text\": \"a) \\nNo beams shall be cut until precautions have\
      \ been taking to prevent it from \\nswinging freely and possibly striking any worker or equipment or any part\
      \ of the structure \\nbeing demolished. \\nb) \\nAll structural steel members shall be lowered from the building\
      \ and shall not be \\nallowed to drop. \\nc) \\nTag lines shall be used on all materials being lowered or\
      \ hoisted up and a \\nstandard signal system shall be used and workmen instructed on the signals. No person\
      \ \\nshall be permitted to ride the load line. \\nd) \\nWhen a derrick or hoisting equipment is used care\
      \ shall be taken to see that the \\nfloor on which it is supported shall be strong enough for the loading.\
      \  If necessary heavy \\nplanking shall be used to distribute the load to floor beams and girders. Overloading\

      \  107.99992370605469,\n        618.002685546875,\n        539.2275390625,\n        742.2382202148438\n  \
      \    ],\n      \"block_number\": 12,\n      \"text\": \"3.11.7 Other elements  \\n \\na) \\nRoof trusses –\
      \ Roof trusses shall be removed to wall plate level by hand methods. \\nSufficient purlins and bracing should\
      \ be retained to ensure stability of the remaining roof \\ntrusses while each individual truss is removed.\
      \ Temporary bracing should be added, \\nwhere necessary, to minimize instability. The end frame opposite to\
      \ the end where \\ndismantling is commenced, or a convenient intermediate frame should be independently \\\

- kind: parameter
  text: Catch platforms must be at least 1.5 m in width, supported by outriggers no more than 3 m apart, feature
    a continuous solid parapet at least 1 m high, sustain a live load of at least 610 kg/m2, and never be used for
    storing or dumping materials.

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"pdf_page\": 29,\n\
      \  \"review_status\": \"pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"176\\n       BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157\
      \       Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &\\n                                                         \
      \                     Technical Specifications\\n\\n\\n            progressed to within three storey of ground\
      \ level, catch platforms will not be considered\\n             necessary.\\n               c)     Catch platforms\
      \ shall not be less than 1.5 m in width measured in a horizontal\\n               direction from the face\
      \ of the structure and shall consist of outriggers supported not more\\n             than 3 m apart. Planks\
      \ shall be laid tight together, without openings between them and\\n             the walls. Catch platforms\
      \ shall be provided with a continuous solid parapet along its\\n              outer edge of at least 1 m height.\
      \ The parapet may be constructed with the same material\\n            as the platform.\\n              d)\
      \ Catch platform shall be capable of sustaining a live load of not less than 610 kg/m2.\\n              e)\
      \ Catch platforms shall neither be used for storing of materials nor dumping of\\n                  materials.\\\
      n\\n             3.11   Demolition of different types of structures and elements\\n\\n             3.11.1\
      \ General.      Structures may be dealt with as masonry, concrete, steel and\\n              timber. The structures\
      \ or their elements shall be dealt with as below, in addition to other\\n             requirements as applicable.\\\
      n\\n            3.11.2 Masonry structures.  Jack Arches – Where tie rods are present between main\\n     \
      \       supporting beams, these should not be cut until after the arch or series of arches in the\\n     \
      \          floor have been removed. Particular care should be exercised and full examination of\\n       \
      \      structure be made before the demolition is commenced. The floor should be demolished\\n           \
      \    in strips parallel to the span of arch rings (at right angles to the main floor beam).\\n\\n\\n\\n  \
      \          Brick Arches\\n\\n              1)   As much dead load as possible may be removed provided it does\
      \ not interfere with\\n                 stability of main arch rings; it should be noted that the load carrying\
      \ capacity of many old\\n             arches relies on the filling between the spandrels. On no account should\
      \ the restraining\\n              influence of the abutments be removed before the dead load of the spandrel\
      \ fill and the\\n             arch rings are removed. The normal sequence of demolition includes the following:\\\
      n                           -    remove the spandrel filling down to the springing line,\\n              \
      \             -    remove the arch rings,\\n                           -    Remove the abutments.\\n\\n  \
      \            2)   A single span arch can be demolished, by hand, by cutting narrow segments\\n           \
      \   progressively from each springing parallel to the span of the arch, until the width of the\\n        \
      \     arch has been reduced to a minimum which can then collapse. Where it is impossible to\\n           \
      \   allow debris to fall to the ground below, centering designed to carry the load should be\\n          \
      \   erected and the arch demolished progressively. The design of the centering should make\\n            \
      \  appropriate allowance for impact.\\n              3)    Where  deliberate collapse  is  feasible the crown\
      \ may be broken working\\n              progressively from the edges to the center.\\n              4)   \
      \  In multi-span arches, before individual spans are removed, lateral restraint should\\n           be provided\
      \ at the springing level. Demolition may be proceeded as for a single span care\\n             being taken\
      \ to demolish the spandrels down to the springing line as the work proceeds.\\n\\n             3.11.3 Reinforced\
      \ concrete\\n\\n              a)  Before commencing demolition, the condition and position of reinforcement\
      \ and\\n                    possibility of lack of  its continuity should be ascertained. Demolition should\
      \ be\\n             commenced by removing partitions, non-load bearing cladding, etc. and similar non-\\n\
      \                   structural elements.\\n              b) Where hand demolition methods are used, the following\
      \ procedures should be used:\\n\\n\\n       Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                 September\
      \ 2026           Section Page 26 of 37\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.99940490722656,\n\
      \        37.728336334228516,\n        526.5408935546875,\n        56.49034118652344\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 0,\n      \"text\": \" BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157              Employer Requirement,\
      \ Scope of Work &  \\n \\n \\n Technical Specifications \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.99996948242188,\n\

      : \"progressed to within three storey of ground level, catch platforms will not be considered \\nnecessary.\
      \ \\nc) \\nCatch platforms shall not be less than 1.5 m in width measured in a horizontal \\ndirection from\
      \ the face of the structure and shall consist of outriggers supported not more \\nthan 3 m apart.  Planks\
      \ shall be laid tight together, without openings between them and \\nthe walls.  Catch platforms shall be\
      \ provided with a continuous solid parapet along its \\nouter edge of at least 1 m height. The parapet may\
      \ be constructed with the same material \\nas the platform. \\nd) Catch platform shall be capable of sustaining\
      \ a live load of not less than 610 kg/m2. \\ne) Catch platforms shall neither be used for storing of materials\
      \ nor dumping of \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        72.00016784667969,\n        200.64288330078125,\n\

      : 7,\n      \"text\": \"3.11.2 Masonry structures. Jack Arches – Where tie rods are present between main \\\
      nsupporting beams, these should not be cut until after the arch or series of arches in the \\nfloor have been\
      \ removed. Particular care should be exercised and full examination of \\nstructure be made before the demolition\
      \ is commenced. The floor should be demolished \\nin strips parallel to the span of arch rings (at right angles\
      \ to the main floor beam). \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        107.99897766113281,\n        365.04266357421875,\n\

      : \" \\n \\nBrick Arches  \\n \\n1) \\nAs much dead load as possible may be removed provided it does not interfere\
      \ with \\nstability of main arch rings; it should be noted that the load carrying capacity of many old \\\
      narches relies on the filling between the spandrels. On no account should the restraining \\ninfluence of\
      \ the abutments be removed before the dead load of the spandrel fill and the \\narch rings are removed. The\
      \ normal sequence of demolition includes the following: \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        107.99861145019531,\n\
      \        478.92266845703125,\n        539.2261352539062,\n        628.4782104492188\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 9,\n      \"text\": \"- \\nremove the spandrel filling down to the springing line, \\n- \\nremove the arch\
      \ rings, \\n- \\nRemove the abutments. \\n \\n2) \\nA single span arch can be demolished, by hand, by cutting\
      \ narrow segments \\nprogressively from each springing parallel to the span of the arch, until the width of\
      \ the \\narch has been reduced to a minimum which can then collapse.  Where it is impossible to \\nallow debris\
      \ to fall to the ground below, centering designed to carry the load should be \\nerected and the arch demolished\
      \ progressively. The design of the centering should make \\nappropriate allowance for impact. \\n3) \\nWhere\
      \ deliberate collapse is feasible the crown may be broken working \\nprogressively from the edges to the center.\
      \ \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.9990234375,\n        630.72265625,\n        540.9058227539062,\n\
      \        678.9981689453125\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 10,\n      \"text\": \"4) \\nIn multi-span arches,\
      \ before individual spans are removed, lateral restraint should \\nbe provided at the springing level. Demolition\
      \ may be proceeded as for a single span care \\nbeing taken to demolish the spandrels down to the springing\
      \ line as the work proceeds.   \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.9990234375,\n     \

- kind: parameter
  text: Sidewalk sheds required for structures over two storeys or 7.5 m high must be capable of sustaining a load
    of 730 kg/m2 (or 1460 kg/m2 if temporary material storage is permitted), maintain a maximum load of 600 kg/m2,
    provide a minimum clearance of 2.4 m, and feature a 1 m high toe board.

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"pdf_page\": 26,\n\
      \  \"review_status\": \"pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"173\\n BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157\
      \       Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &\\n                                                         \
      \              Technical Specifications\\n\\n\\n\\n                                   iii.  While the mechanical\
      \ device is in operation no workmen shall be allowed\\n                       to enter the building being\
      \ demolished;\\n\\n                        iv. The device shall be so located as to avoid falling debris;\
      \ and\\n\\n                     v. The device when being used shall not cause any damage to adjacent\\n  \
      \                      structure, power line, other services, etc.\\n\\n       3.7    Protection of the public\\\
      n\\n       Protection of the public before and during demolition is important and the following points\\n\
      \       should be kept in mind:\\n\\n       a) Every sidewalk or road adjacent to the work shall be closed\
      \ or protected. All main\\n           roads, which are open to the public shall be kept open to the public\
      \ clear and\\n          unobstructed at all times.\\n       b)  Children and public shall be kept out of the\
      \ building and the adjoining yards.\\n        c)   If the structure to be demolished is more than two-storied\
      \ or 7.5 m high, measured\\n          from the sidewalk or street which cannot be closed or safely diverted,\
      \ and the\\n           horizontal distance from the inside edge of the side walk to the structure is 4.5 m\
      \ or\\n            less, a substantial side walk shed shall be constructed over the entire length of the\\\
      n          sidewalk adjacent to the structure of sufficient width with a view to accommodating\\n        \
      \   the pedestrian  traffic without causing congestion. The sidewalk shall be lighted\\n             sufficiently\
      \ to ensure safety at all times.\\n       d) The structure to be demolished/dismantled must have barricades,\
      \ which shall be of\\n            plain MS Sheet of 16 gauge fixed with steel frame, painting, with synthetic\
      \ enamel\\n           paint of approved color, quality and brand, writing letter and logo of metro including\\\
      n         maintenance of the same, duly cleaning the same frequently, arrangement for blinker\\n         \
      \    light as per requirement. During demolition barricades has to be kept continuously.\\n         Removal\
      \ of barricade will not be permitted till completion of demolition, dismantling\\n            of properties.\\\
      n       e) A toe board at least 1 m high above the roof of the side walk shed shall be provided\\n       \
      \  on the outside edge and ends of the sidewalk shed. Such boards may be vertical or\\n            inclined\
      \ outward at not more than 45 degrees.\\n           f)  Except where the roof of a side walk shed solidly\
      \ abuts the structure, the face of the\\n          sidewalk shed towards the building shall be completely\
      \ closed by providing sheeting\\n                  / planking to prevent falling material penetrating into\
      \ the shed.\\n       g) The roof of the sidewalk shed shall be capable of sustaining a load of 730 kg/m2.\\\
      n          Only in exceptional cases, say due to lack of other space, the storing of the material\\n     \
      \    on a sidewalk shed may be permitted in which case the shed shall be designed for a\\n          load of\
      \ 1460 kg/m2. Roof of sidewalk shed shall be designed taking into account the\\n          impact of the falling\
      \ debris. By frequent removal of loads it shall be ensured that the\\n        maximum load, at any time, on\
      \ the roof of the shed is not more than 600 kg/m2. The\\n           height of the sidewalk shed shall be such\
      \ as to give minimum clearance of 2.4 m.\\n       h) Sidewalk shed openings, for loading purposes, shall be\
      \ kept closed at all times except\\n           during actual loading operations.\\n            i)  The deck\
      \ flooring of the sidewalk shed shall consist of plank of not less than 50 mm\\n           thickness closely\
      \ laid and deck made watertight.\\n            j)   All members of the shed shall be adequately braced and\
      \ connected to resist\\n          displacement of members or distortion of framework.\\n        k) When the\
      \ horizontal distance from the inside edge of the sidewalk to the structure is\\n         more than 4.5 m\
      \ and less than 7.5 m, a sidewalk shed or fence may be built or in their\\n          place a substantial railing\
      \ shall be constructed on the inside of the sidewalk or\\n\\n\\n\\n\\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.\
      \                 September 2026           Section Page 23 of 37\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"\

- kind: parameter
  text: Debris dropped through floor openings without chutes must ensure the total cut area in intermediate floors
    does not exceed 25 per cent of the floor area, and intermediate floor openings must be protected by standard
    railings and toe boards.
  evidence_ids:
  - Jdba3f2606eeb9cce
  relation_type: none
  explicitness: not_a_relation
  from_id: ''
  to_id: ''
  id: C6b7efdb0ea151f70
  citations:
  - id: Jdba3f2606eeb9cce
    okf_path: OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol2/vol2_part4A.md
    okf_sha256: 520ac8689dbcfe1f3be2ed99dafda8715eb436313221c6ca77dd66080cd2f726
    heading: 'Employer Requirement, Scope of Work and Technical Specifications – Part 4A: Specifications for Demolition/Dismantling
      Works'
    source_document: Vol2DLNP1.pdf
    source_sha256: e44cc4945aec704af8350cca6388b3fe3b741d76577f7da70cee6a836662582b
    pdf_pages:
    - 31
    sheet_cells: ''
    json_path: parsed_json/vol2/part_4a_demolition/demolition_clauses.json
    json_sha256: e15324023aacf8bda736e3cf21e3a65735deb4ba19d458a5cd2099534b681a2e
    json_pointer: /source_page_context/8
    json_record_id: null
    source_review_status: pending_manual_review
    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"pdf_page\": 31,\n\
      \  \"review_status\": \"pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"178\\n       BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157\
      \       Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &\\n                                                         \
      \                     Technical Specifications\\n\\n\\n              b)   Cantilevers – A  cantilever  type\
      \  of  construction depends on  the  balancing\\n            superimposed structure for its stability. Canopies,\
      \ cornices, staircases, balconies should\\n           be demolished or supported before the balancing load\
      \ is removed.\\n               c)  Heavy floor beams – Heavy bulks of timber should be supported before cutting\
      \ at\\n             the extremities and should then be lowered to a safe working place.\\n\\n            \
      \ 3.12  Removal of materials\\n\\n            3.12.1  General.     Removal of dismantled materials should\
      \ be done carefully; they\\n         may be thrown / lowered to the ground. The materials shall preferably\
      \ be dumped inside\\n            the building. Normally such materials shall be lowered to the ground or to\
      \ the top of the\\n            sidewalk shed where provided by means of ropes or suitable tackles.\\n    \
      \        3.12.2  Through chutes\\n\\n               a.   Wooden or metal shall be provided for removal of\
      \ materials.  The chutes shall\\n              preferably be provided at the center of the building for efficient\
      \ disposal of debris.\\n\\n                   b.  Chutes if provided at an angle of more than 45 degree from\
      \ the horizontal shall\\n                 be entirely enclosed on all sides, except for opening at or about\
      \ the floor level for\\n                     receiving materials.\\n                    c.  Opening for chutes\
      \ shall not exceed 1.20 m in height measured along the wall of\\n                    the chute and in all\
      \ storeys below the top floor such opening shall be kept closed\\n                when not in use.\\n    \
      \               d. To prevent the descending material attaining a dangerous speed, the chute shall\\n    \
      \                not extend in an unbroken line for more than two storeys. A gate or step shall be\\n    \
      \                provided with suitable means of closing at the bottom of each chute to stop the\\n      \
      \               flow of materials.\\n                   e.  Chutes at an angle less than 45 degree to the\
      \ horizontal may be left open on the\\n                   upper side provided that at the point where such\
      \ chute discharges into the chute\\n                    steeper than 45 degree to the horizontal, the top\
      \ of the steeper chute shall be\\n                  boarded over to prevent the escape of materials.\\n  \
      \                          f.  Any opening into which workmen dump debris at the top of the chute shall be\\\
      n                  guarded by a substantial guard rail extending at least 1 m above the level of the\\n  \
      \                      floor or other surface on which men stand to dump the materials into the chute.\\n\
      \                   g. A toe board or bumper not less than 50 mm thick and 150 mm high shall be\\n       \
      \             provided at each chute opening, if the required material is dumped from the wheel\\n       \
      \             barrows. Any space between the chute and the edge of the opening in the floor\\n           \
      \         through which it passes shall be solidly planked over.\\n\\n             3.12.3 Through openings\\\
      n\\n                  a)  Debris may also be dropped through holes in the floor without the use of chutes.\\\
      n                       In such a case the total area of the hole cut in the intermediate floor, one which\\\
      n                         lies between floor that is being demolished and the storage floor shall not exceed\\\
      n                 25 per cent of such floor area.  It shall be ensured that the storage floor is of\\n   \
      \               adequate strength to withstand the impact of the falling material.\\n                  b)\
      \ Openings in all floors below the floor from which materials are being removed,\\n                      \
      \ shall be protected by standard railings and toe boards (see IS 4912: 1978) or\\n                     preferably\
      \ planked over if the holes are not being used for dumping materials.\\n                   c)  All intermediate\
      \ floor openings for passage of materials shall be completely closed\\n                     with barricades\
      \ or guard rails not less than 1 m high and at a distance of not less\\n                   than 1 m from the\
      \ edge of the general opening. No barricades or guard rails shall\\n                 be removed until the\
      \ storey immediately above has been demolished down to the\\n                        floor line and all debris\
      \ cleared from the floor.\\n\\n\\n\\n\\n       Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                 September\
      \ 2026           Section Page 28 of 37\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.99940490722656,\n\
      \        37.728336334228516,\n        526.5408935546875,\n        56.49034118652344\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 0,\n      \"text\": \" BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157              Employer Requirement,\
      \ Scope of Work &  \\n \\n \\n Technical Specifications \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.99996948242188,\n\
      \        787.1278076171875,\n        538.54248046875,\n        795.56982421875\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 1,\n      \"text\": \"Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd. \\n \\nSeptember 2026                    Section\
      \ Page 28 of 37 \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.99996948242188,\n        798.4948120117188,\n\
      \        538.4083251953125,\n        826.9197998046875\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 2,\n      \"text\"\
      : \" \\n \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        72.00048828125,\n        74.16320037841797,\n\
      \        539.2273559570312,\n        147.7585906982422\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 3,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"b) \\nCantilevers – A cantilever type of construction depends on the balancing \\nsuperimposed structure\
      \ for its stability.  Canopies, cornices, staircases, balconies should \\nbe demolished or supported before\
      \ the balancing load is removed. \\nc) \\nHeavy floor beams – Heavy bulks of timber should be supported before\
      \ cutting at \\nthe extremities and should then be lowered to a safe working place. \\n \\n\"\n    },\n  \

- kind: parameter
  text: Chutes for material removal at an angle of more than 45 degrees must be entirely enclosed on all sides with
    openings not exceeding 1.20 m in height, restricted to a maximum of two storeys without an unbroken line, and
    fitted with a gate or step at the bottom.

      \       Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &\\n                                                         \
      \                     Technical Specifications\\n\\n\\n              b)   Cantilevers – A  cantilever  type\
      \  of  construction depends on  the  balancing\\n            superimposed structure for its stability. Canopies,\
      \ cornices, staircases, balconies should\\n           be demolished or supported before the balancing load\
      \ is removed.\\n               c)  Heavy floor beams – Heavy bulks of timber should be supported before cutting\
      \ at\\n             the extremities and should then be lowered to a safe working place.\\n\\n            \
      \ 3.12  Removal of materials\\n\\n            3.12.1  General.     Removal of dismantled materials should\
      \ be done carefully; they\\n         may be thrown / lowered to the ground. The materials shall preferably\
      \ be dumped inside\\n            the building. Normally such materials shall be lowered to the ground or to\
      \ the top of the\\n            sidewalk shed where provided by means of ropes or suitable tackles.\\n    \
      \        3.12.2  Through chutes\\n\\n               a.   Wooden or metal shall be provided for removal of\
      \ materials.  The chutes shall\\n              preferably be provided at the center of the building for efficient\
      \ disposal of debris.\\n\\n                   b.  Chutes if provided at an angle of more than 45 degree from\
      \ the horizontal shall\\n                 be entirely enclosed on all sides, except for opening at or about\
      \ the floor level for\\n                     receiving materials.\\n                    c.  Opening for chutes\
      \ shall not exceed 1.20 m in height measured along the wall of\\n                    the chute and in all\
      \ storeys below the top floor such opening shall be kept closed\\n                when not in use.\\n    \
      \               d. To prevent the descending material attaining a dangerous speed, the chute shall\\n    \
      \                not extend in an unbroken line for more than two storeys. A gate or step shall be\\n    \
      \                provided with suitable means of closing at the bottom of each chute to stop the\\n      \
      \               flow of materials.\\n                   e.  Chutes at an angle less than 45 degree to the\
      \ horizontal may be left open on the\\n                   upper side provided that at the point where such\
      \ chute discharges into the chute\\n                    steeper than 45 degree to the horizontal, the top\
      \ of the steeper chute shall be\\n                  boarded over to prevent the escape of materials.\\n  \

      n                  guarded by a substantial guard rail extending at least 1 m above the level of the\\n  \
      \                      floor or other surface on which men stand to dump the materials into the chute.\\n\

      n                       In such a case the total area of the hole cut in the intermediate floor, one which\\\
      n                         lies between floor that is being demolished and the storage floor shall not exceed\\\
      n                 25 per cent of such floor area.  It shall be ensured that the storage floor is of\\n   \

      \ Openings in all floors below the floor from which materials are being removed,\\n                      \
      \ shall be protected by standard railings and toe boards (see IS 4912: 1978) or\\n                     preferably\
      \ planked over if the holes are not being used for dumping materials.\\n                   c)  All intermediate\
      \ floor openings for passage of materials shall be completely closed\\n                     with barricades\
      \ or guard rails not less than 1 m high and at a distance of not less\\n                   than 1 m from the\
      \ edge of the general opening. No barricades or guard rails shall\\n                 be removed until the\
      \ storey immediately above has been demolished down to the\\n                        floor line and all debris\
      \ cleared from the floor.\\n\\n\\n\\n\\n       Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                 September\

- kind: summary
  text: Technical specifications outline precise procedures for various structural elements including jack arches,
    brick arches, reinforced concrete beams and columns, pre-cast concrete units, pre-stressed concrete, steel members
    requiring tag lines and controlled lowering, and roof trusses.

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"pdf_page\": 29,\n\
      \  \"review_status\": \"pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"176\\n       BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157\
      \       Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &\\n                                                         \
      \                     Technical Specifications\\n\\n\\n            progressed to within three storey of ground\
      \ level, catch platforms will not be considered\\n             necessary.\\n               c)     Catch platforms\
      \ shall not be less than 1.5 m in width measured in a horizontal\\n               direction from the face\
      \ of the structure and shall consist of outriggers supported not more\\n             than 3 m apart. Planks\
      \ shall be laid tight together, without openings between them and\\n             the walls. Catch platforms\
      \ shall be provided with a continuous solid parapet along its\\n              outer edge of at least 1 m height.\
      \ The parapet may be constructed with the same material\\n            as the platform.\\n              d)\
      \ Catch platform shall be capable of sustaining a live load of not less than 610 kg/m2.\\n              e)\
      \ Catch platforms shall neither be used for storing of materials nor dumping of\\n                  materials.\\\
      n\\n             3.11   Demolition of different types of structures and elements\\n\\n             3.11.1\
      \ General.      Structures may be dealt with as masonry, concrete, steel and\\n              timber. The structures\
      \ or their elements shall be dealt with as below, in addition to other\\n             requirements as applicable.\\\
      n\\n            3.11.2 Masonry structures.  Jack Arches – Where tie rods are present between main\\n     \
      \       supporting beams, these should not be cut until after the arch or series of arches in the\\n     \
      \          floor have been removed. Particular care should be exercised and full examination of\\n       \
      \      structure be made before the demolition is commenced. The floor should be demolished\\n           \
      \    in strips parallel to the span of arch rings (at right angles to the main floor beam).\\n\\n\\n\\n  \
      \          Brick Arches\\n\\n              1)   As much dead load as possible may be removed provided it does\
      \ not interfere with\\n                 stability of main arch rings; it should be noted that the load carrying\
      \ capacity of many old\\n             arches relies on the filling between the spandrels. On no account should\
      \ the restraining\\n              influence of the abutments be removed before the dead load of the spandrel\
      \ fill and the\\n             arch rings are removed. The normal sequence of demolition includes the following:\\\
      n                           -    remove the spandrel filling down to the springing line,\\n              \
      \             -    remove the arch rings,\\n                           -    Remove the abutments.\\n\\n  \
      \            2)   A single span arch can be demolished, by hand, by cutting narrow segments\\n           \
      \   progressively from each springing parallel to the span of the arch, until the width of the\\n        \
      \     arch has been reduced to a minimum which can then collapse. Where it is impossible to\\n           \
      \   allow debris to fall to the ground below, centering designed to carry the load should be\\n          \
      \   erected and the arch demolished progressively. The design of the centering should make\\n            \
      \  appropriate allowance for impact.\\n              3)    Where  deliberate collapse  is  feasible the crown\
      \ may be broken working\\n              progressively from the edges to the center.\\n              4)   \
      \  In multi-span arches, before individual spans are removed, lateral restraint should\\n           be provided\
      \ at the springing level. Demolition may be proceeded as for a single span care\\n             being taken\
      \ to demolish the spandrels down to the springing line as the work proceeds.\\n\\n             3.11.3 Reinforced\
      \ concrete\\n\\n              a)  Before commencing demolition, the condition and position of reinforcement\
      \ and\\n                    possibility of lack of  its continuity should be ascertained. Demolition should\
      \ be\\n             commenced by removing partitions, non-load bearing cladding, etc. and similar non-\\n\
      \                   structural elements.\\n              b) Where hand demolition methods are used, the following\
      \ procedures should be used:\\n\\n\\n       Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                 September\
      \ 2026           Section Page 26 of 37\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.99940490722656,\n\

      : \"progressed to within three storey of ground level, catch platforms will not be considered \\nnecessary.\
      \ \\nc) \\nCatch platforms shall not be less than 1.5 m in width measured in a horizontal \\ndirection from\
      \ the face of the structure and shall consist of outriggers supported not more \\nthan 3 m apart.  Planks\
      \ shall be laid tight together, without openings between them and \\nthe walls.  Catch platforms shall be\
      \ provided with a continuous solid parapet along its \\nouter edge of at least 1 m height. The parapet may\
      \ be constructed with the same material \\nas the platform. \\nd) Catch platform shall be capable of sustaining\
      \ a live load of not less than 610 kg/m2. \\ne) Catch platforms shall neither be used for storing of materials\
      \ nor dumping of \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        72.00016784667969,\n        200.64288330078125,\n\

      : 7,\n      \"text\": \"3.11.2 Masonry structures. Jack Arches – Where tie rods are present between main \\\
      nsupporting beams, these should not be cut until after the arch or series of arches in the \\nfloor have been\

      \ is commenced. The floor should be demolished \\nin strips parallel to the span of arch rings (at right angles\

      : \" \\n \\nBrick Arches  \\n \\n1) \\nAs much dead load as possible may be removed provided it does not interfere\

      \        478.92266845703125,\n        539.2261352539062,\n        628.4782104492188\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 9,\n      \"text\": \"- \\nremove the spandrel filling down to the springing line, \\n- \\nremove the arch\
      \ rings, \\n- \\nRemove the abutments. \\n \\n2) \\nA single span arch can be demolished, by hand, by cutting\
      \ narrow segments \\nprogressively from each springing parallel to the span of the arch, until the width of\

      \ to fall to the ground below, centering designed to carry the load should be \\nerected and the arch demolished\
      \ progressively. The design of the centering should make \\nappropriate allowance for impact. \\n3) \\nWhere\
      \ deliberate collapse is feasible the crown may be broken working \\nprogressively from the edges to the center.\
      \ \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.9990234375,\n        630.72265625,\n        540.9058227539062,\n\
      \        678.9981689453125\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 10,\n      \"text\": \"4) \\nIn multi-span arches,\
      \ before individual spans are removed, lateral restraint should \\nbe provided at the springing level. Demolition\
      \ may be proceeded as for a single span care \\nbeing taken to demolish the spandrels down to the springing\
      \ line as the work proceeds.   \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.9990234375,\n     \

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"pdf_page\": 30,\n\
      \  \"review_status\": \"pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"177\\n BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157\
      \       Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &\\n                                                         \
      \              Technical Specifications\\n\\n\\n\\n           1) Beams – For beams supporting rope should\
      \ be attached to the beam. Then the\\n              concrete should be removed from both ends by pneumatic\
      \ drill or cutting tools\\n            and the reinforcement exposed. The reinforcement should then be cut\
      \ in which a\\n           way as to allow the beam to be lowered under control to the floor.\\n          \
      \ 2) Columns – For columns reinforcement should be exposed at the base after\\n               restraining\
      \ wire guy ropes have been placed around the member at the top. The\\n              reinforcement should then\
      \ be cut in such a way as to allow the column be pulled\\n           down to the floor under control.\\n \
      \          3) Walls – Reinforced concrete walls should be cut into strips and demolished as for\\n       \
      \      columns.\\n           4) Suspended floors and roofs – where ribbed construction is used, the principle\\\
      n                of design and method of construction should be ascertained before demolition.\\n        \
      \    Care should be taken not to cut the ribs inadvertently.\\n\\n       3.11.4 Pre-cast reinforced concrete\\\
      n\\n       a) Pre-cast reinforced concrete units in a structure are normally held in position by the\\n  \
      \         strength of the joints in-situ or on supporting walls, etc. As such before starting on\\n      \
      \     demolition the joint structures or the supporting mechanisms shall be studied and\\n          understood.\\\
      n       b) In devising the following demolition sequences, due precaution shall be taken to avoid\\n     \
      \      toppling over of the prefabricated units or any other part of the structure and wherever\\n       \
      \   necessary temporary supports shall be provided.\\n\\n       3.11.5 Pre-stressed concrete.     Before \
      \ commencing  of  the  demolition  work\\n        involving such structures advice of an expert engineer should\
      \ be obtained.\\n\\n       3.11.6 Steel\\n\\n       a)    No beams shall be cut until precautions have been\
      \ taking to prevent  it from\\n       swinging freely and possibly striking any worker or equipment or any\
      \ part of the structure\\n       being demolished.\\n       b)      All structural steel members shall be\
      \ lowered from the building and shall not be\\n       allowed to drop.\\n        c)    Tag lines shall be\
      \ used on  all materials being lowered or hoisted up and a\\n       standard signal system shall be used and\
      \ workmen instructed on the signals. No person\\n        shall be permitted to ride the load line.\\n    \
      \   d)    When a derrick or hoisting equipment is used care shall be taken to see that the\\n        floor\
      \ on which it is supported shall be strong enough for the loading.  If necessary heavy\\n       planking shall\
      \ be used to distribute the load to floor beams and girders. Overloading /\\n       overturning of the equipment\
      \ shall be avoided.\\n\\n       3.11.7 Other elements\\n\\n       a)     Roof trusses – Roof trusses shall\
      \ be removed to wall plate level by hand methods.\\n        Sufficient purlins and bracing should be retained\
      \ to ensure stability of the remaining roof\\n       trusses while each individual truss is removed. Temporary\
      \ bracing should be added,\\n      where necessary, to minimize instability. The end frame opposite to the\
      \ end where\\n       dismantling is commenced, or a convenient intermediate frame should be independently\\\
      n      and securely guyed in both directions before work starts. On no account should the\\n      bottom tie\
      \ of a truss be cut until the principal rafters are prevented from making outward\\n      movement.\\n\\n\\\
      n\\n\\n\\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                 September 2026           Section Page 27 of\
      \ 37\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.99940490722656,\n        37.728336334228516,\n\
      \        526.5408935546875,\n        56.49034118652344\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 0,\n      \"text\"\
      : \" BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157              Employer Requirement, Scope of Work & \
      \ \\n \\n \\n Technical Specifications \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.99996948242188,\n\

      : \"1) Beams – For beams supporting rope should be attached to the beam. Then the \\nconcrete should be removed\
      \ from both ends by pneumatic drill or cutting tools \\nand the reinforcement exposed. The reinforcement should\

      \ – For columns reinforcement should be exposed at the base after \\nrestraining wire guy ropes have been\
      \ placed around the member at the top.  The \\nreinforcement should then be cut in such a way as to allow\
      \ the column be pulled \\ndown to the floor under control. \\n3) Walls – Reinforced concrete walls should\
      \ be cut into strips and demolished as for \\ncolumns. \\n4) Suspended floors and roofs – where ribbed construction\

      \ \\nCare should be taken not to cut the ribs inadvertently. \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n\

      \ etc.  As such before starting on \\ndemolition the joint structures or the supporting mechanisms shall be\
      \ studied and \\nunderstood. \\nb)  In devising the following demolition sequences, due precaution shall be\
      \ taken to avoid \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        72.00044250488281,\n        352.4427490234375,\n\

      : \"toppling over of the prefabricated units or any other part of the structure and wherever \\nnecessary\
      \ temporary supports shall be provided. \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        71.99996948242188,\n\
      \        390.24273681640625,\n        539.22802734375,\n        426.0382385253906\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 9,\n      \"text\": \"3.11.5 Pre-stressed concrete. \\nBefore commencing of the demolition work \\ninvolving\
      \ such structures advice of an expert engineer should be obtained. \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\"\
      : [\n        71.99996948242188,\n        428.1627502441406,\n        176.46902465820312,\n        451.23822021484375\n\

      bbox\": [\n        72.0,\n        453.60272216796875,\n        539.2273559570312,\n        615.7582397460938\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 11,\n      \"text\": \"a) \\nNo beams shall be cut until precautions have\
      \ been taking to prevent it from \\nswinging freely and possibly striking any worker or equipment or any part\
      \ of the structure \\nbeing demolished. \\nb) \\nAll structural steel members shall be lowered from the building\
      \ and shall not be \\nallowed to drop. \\nc) \\nTag lines shall be used on all materials being lowered or\
      \ hoisted up and a \\nstandard signal system shall be used and workmen instructed on the signals. No person\
      \ \\nshall be permitted to ride the load line. \\nd) \\nWhen a derrick or hoisting equipment is used care\
      \ shall be taken to see that the \\nfloor on which it is supported shall be strong enough for the loading.\
      \  If necessary heavy \\nplanking shall be used to distribute the load to floor beams and girders. Overloading\
      \ / \\noverturning of the equipment shall be avoided. \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n      \
      \  107.99992370605469,\n        618.002685546875,\n        539.2275390625,\n        742.2382202148438\n  \
      \    ],\n      \"block_number\": 12,\n      \"text\": \"3.11.7 Other elements  \\n \\na) \\nRoof trusses –\
      \ Roof trusses shall be removed to wall plate level by hand methods. \\nSufficient purlins and bracing should\
      \ be retained to ensure stability of the remaining roof \\ntrusses while each individual truss is removed.\
      \ Temporary bracing should be added, \\nwhere necessary, to minimize instability. The end frame opposite to\
      \ the end where \\ndismantling is commenced, or a convenient intermediate frame should be independently \\\

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"pdf_page\": 32,\n\
      \  \"review_status\": \"pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"179\\n BMRCL/Phase-3/JPN-MRS/Demolition/DLN-P1/2026/157\
      \       Employer Requirement, Scope of Work &\\n                                                         \
      \              Technical Specifications\\n\\n\\n           d) When cutting a hole in an intermediate floor,\
      \ between the storage floor and the\\n                 floor which is being demolished, makes the intermediate\
      \ floor or any portion of it\\n              unsafe, then such intermediate floor shall be properly shored.\
      \  It shall also be\\n             ensured that the supporting walls are not kept without adequate lateral\
      \ restraints.\\n\\n       3.13  References\\n\\n       Other Indian Standards on the subject of safety of\
      \ workers are as follows:\\n\\n                 IS No.                                        Title\\n   \
      \     3696 (Part 1): 1987      Safety code of scaffolds and ladders Part 1 Scaffolds\\n\\n        4014 (Part\
      \ 2): 1967     Code of practice for steel tubular scaffolding ; Part 2 Safety\\n                         \
      \          regulations for scaffolding\\n\\n         3764: 1992           Code of safety for excavation work\
      \ (first revision)\\n\\n         7969: 1975             Safety code for handling and storage of building materials.\\\
      n\\n         13415: 1992          Code  of  safety  for  protective  barriers  in and around\\n          \

- kind: summary
  text: Before beginning demolition operations, the contractor must conduct a signed joint inventory of serviceable
    materials, establish a day-to-day salvage account register, and ensure safe custody of all serviceable and salvageable
    items.
  evidence_ids:
  - J02d1919d27928b39
  relation_type: none
  explicitness: not_a_relation
  from_id: ''
  to_id: ''
  id: C82ad118df81449e7
  citations:
  - id: J02d1919d27928b39
    okf_path: OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol2/vol2_part4A.md
    okf_sha256: 520ac8689dbcfe1f3be2ed99dafda8715eb436313221c6ca77dd66080cd2f726
    heading: Clause 3 — Serviceable and Unserviceable Materials
    source_document: Vol2DLNP1.pdf
    source_sha256: e44cc4945aec704af8350cca6388b3fe3b741d76577f7da70cee6a836662582b
    pdf_pages:
    - 23
    sheet_cells: ''
    json_path: parsed_json/vol2/part_4a_demolition/demolition_clauses.json
    json_sha256: e15324023aacf8bda736e3cf21e3a65735deb4ba19d458a5cd2099534b681a2e
    json_pointer: /clauses/2/fragments/0
    json_record_id: null
    source_review_status: pending_manual_review
    quote: "{\n  \"pdf_page\": 23,\n  \"text\": \"3.  Serviceable and unserviceable materials\\n          3.1 Inventory.\
      \    Before  dismantling/demolition  operations  are  undertaken  by  the\\n              contractor, inventory\
      \ of all materials, fittings and fixtures (except hidden materials) which\\n             are considered useful\
      \ shall be made and signed by the Employers representative and the\\n              contractor, wherever the\
      \ operations are entrusted to a contractor.\\n\\n          3.2 Serviceable materials.    Any material which\
      \ is in the opinion of the Employer’s\\n              representative could be reused or otherwise useful will\
      \ be considered as serviceable. The\\n             serviceable/salvageable items (for which ‘owner losing\
      \ structure totally/partially’ has no\\n               interest) will belong to the contractor, for which\
      \ the contractor shall give credit to BMRCL\\n           (Summary Sheet)\\n\\n          3.3 Unserviceable\
      \ materials.  Any material declared by the Employer’s representative are\\n          not serviceable shall\
      \ be considered as unserviceable. A register shall be opened at the work\\n            site to show day-to-day\
      \ account of the turnout of salvaged materials. The register shall also\\n           indicate whether dismantled\
      \ materials are properly stacked or wasted. The contractor shall\\n        be responsible for the safe custody\
      \ of all serviceable materials.\\n\\n          3.4 Hazards in demolition.     Demolition of any structure\
      \ is, inherently, more hazardous\\n         than the construction or erection of the same. From the point\
      \ of view of safety, the conditions\\n          usually encountered while dismantling a structure, whatever\
      \  its magnitude, do not lend\\n         themselves to the degree of control possible in the construction\
      \ operations, where more\\n          stable conditions are generally obtainable.  It is  all the more imperative;\
      \ therefore, that\\n         adequate attention is paid to planning and execution of demolition work, in its\
      \ various stages,\\n         so as to minimize the risk of accidents and injuries to the personnel engaged\
      \ in demolition\\n          operations. It has therefore become necessary to lay down certain safety procedures,\
      \ which\\n         along with a planned programme could ensure adequate safety, particularly with the\\n \
      \        involvement  of management, supervisors and workers. The demolition work  shall be\\n         preceded\
      \ in such a way that:\\n\\n              a)       it causes least damage and nuisance to other parts of the\
      \ same building, adjoining\\n                     building and the residents of the building and the members\
      \ of the public ; and\\n\\n              b)       it satisfies all safety requirements to avoid mishaps and\
      \ accidents.\"\n}"
    page_basis: Parsed record provenance; not independently PDF-verified

- kind: parameter
  text: Continuous protective barricades constructed of plain MS Sheet of 16 gauge fixed with steel frames, painted
    with synthetic enamel paint of approved color and brand, featuring metro lettering/logo, blinker lights, and
    frequent cleaning, must be maintained throughout the demolition process.
  evidence_ids:
  - Je57f51de5f815015
  - Jb29804ec5d4e0387
  - J0e7ba085aa6e53a9
  relation_type: none
  explicitness: not_a_relation

  citations:
  - id: Je57f51de5f815015
    okf_path: OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol2/vol2_part4A.md
    okf_sha256: 520ac8689dbcfe1f3be2ed99dafda8715eb436313221c6ca77dd66080cd2f726
    heading: Clause 3 — Serviceable and Unserviceable Materials
    source_document: Vol2DLNP1.pdf
    source_sha256: e44cc4945aec704af8350cca6388b3fe3b741d76577f7da70cee6a836662582b
    pdf_pages:
    - 24
    sheet_cells: ''
    json_path: parsed_json/vol2/part_4a_demolition/demolition_clauses.json
    json_sha256: e15324023aacf8bda736e3cf21e3a65735deb4ba19d458a5cd2099534b681a2e
    json_pointer: /clauses/2/fragments/1
    json_record_id: null
    source_review_status: pending_manual_review
    quote: "{\n  \"pdf_page\": 24,\n  \"text\": \"3.5    Planning.    Before beginning the actual work of demolition,\
      \ the contractor shall\\n        carefully study the structure, which is to be pulled down and also of its\
      \ surroundings. This\\n        shall include the following:\\n\\n        a) The manner in which the various\
      \ parts of buildings are supported and how far the\\n          stage by stage demolition would affect the\
      \ safety of the rest of the same structure\\n         and the adjoining structure;\\n        b) A definite\
      \ plan and procedure of demolition work shall be prepared, taking into\\n          account the loads on various\
      \ structural parts and their supports;\\n        c)  Before commencement of each stage of demolition, the\
      \ supervisor shall brief the\\n         workmen in detail regarding the safety aspects to be kept in view;\\\
      n        d)  Ensure that the demolition conditions do not, at any stage, enhance the nuisance\\n         \
      \  value of demolition work on the use of adjacent buildings;\\n        e) No structure or part of the structure\
      \ or any floor or temporary support or scaffold, side\\n            wall or any device for equipment shall\
      \ be loaded in excess of the safe load bearing\\n            capacity, in its then existing condition; and\\\
      n           f)   Stairs and stair railings, passage ways and ladders shall be left in place as long as\\n\
      \            possible. These should be maintained in a safe condition.\\n\\n       3.6    Precautions and\
      \ protective measures before starting demolition work\\n      The following precautions and protective measures\
      \ shall be taken before commencement\\n        of demolition work:\\n\\n        (a)   On every demolition\
      \ job, danger signals shall be conspicuously posted all around\\n       the structure and all doors, openings\
      \ giving access to structures shall be kept barricaded\\n        or manned except during the actual passage\
      \ of workmen or equipment. However,\\n        provision shall be made for at least two independent exits for\
      \ escape of workmen during\\n      any emergency.\\n        (b)   Walkways and passageways shall be provided\
      \ for the use of the workmen who\\n        shall be instructed to use them and all such walkways and passageways\
      \ shall be kept\\n       adequately lighted, free from all debris and other materials.\\n        (c)    Where\
      \ in any work of demolition it is imperative, because of existing danger, to\\n      ensure that no unauthorized\
      \ person shall enter the site of demolition outside working\\n       hours, watchmen shall be employed all\
      \ 24 hrs.in a day. In addition to watching the site\\n      he shall also be responsible for maintaining all\
      \ signs, notices, lights, barricades, etc.\\n       During nights, red lights shall be placed on or about\
      \ the barricades.\\n        (d)    The power on all electrical service lines shall be shutoff and all such\
      \ lines cut or\\n       disconnected at or outside the property line. The only exception would be any power\
      \ lines\\n       required for the demolition work itself.  Prior to cutting of/disconnecting such lines, the\\\
      n      necessary approval of the Authority shall be obtained and beneficiaries intimated in\\n      advance.\\\
      n        (e)    The structure to be demolished/dismantled must have barricades, which shall be\\n        of\
      \ plain MS Sheet of 16 gauge fixed with steel frame, painting, with synthetic enamel paint\\n        of approved\
      \  color,  quality and brand,  writing  letter and logo  of metro  including\\n      maintenance of the same,\
      \ duly cleaning the same frequently, arrangement for blinker\\n         light as per requirement. During demolition\
      \ barricades has to be kept continuously.\\n      Removal of barricade will not be permitted  till completion\
      \ of demolition, dismantling of\\n       properties\\n\\n          (f)   All mains and meters of the building\
      \ shall be removed or protected from damage.\\n        (g)  All gas, water, steam and other service lines\
      \ shall be shutoff and capped or otherwise\\n            controlled at or outside the property line.\\n  \
      \      (h)  If a structure to be demolished has been partially wrecked by fire, explosion or other\\n    \
      \       catastrophe, the walls and damaged roofs shall be shored and braced suitably.\"\n}"
    page_basis: Parsed record provenance; not independently PDF-verified
